# Konkourix Canonical Curriculum

## Status and authority

This document contains a lossless curriculum transcription from `cori.docx`. The DOCX, governed by [CURRICULUM_SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md](../CURRICULUM_SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md), is authoritative. Architecture terms, identifiers, lifecycle rules, and semantic type mappings in this document are retained projection metadata; they are not educational source content and do not override the DOCX.

The source contains two complementary views:

1. a complete high-level matrix of shared and field-specific subjects for grades 10, 11, and 12; and
2. detailed trees for selected subjects, plus several cross-grade thematic trees.

The high-level matrix is authoritative for subject availability. A detailed tree is authoritative only for the nodes it explicitly contains. Absence of a detailed tree must not be interpreted as removal of the subject.

## Historical projection metadata: hierarchy

The following owning-tree projection was introduced after the AssessmentAttempt checkpoint. It is not an authoritative fixed-depth taxonomy and is not approved runtime recovery architecture.

```text
Curriculum root
└── Field / major scope
    └── Grade
        └── Subject
            └── Chapter
                └── Topic
                    └── Concept
                        └── Sub-concept (only when the source has a deeper level)
```

`دروس مشترک پایه‌های دهم، یازدهم و دوازدهم` is the source-defined shared scope. The other scopes are `رشته ریاضی‌فیزیک`, `رشته علوم تجربی`, and `رشته علوم انسانی`. “Shared” is therefore a scope in the owning tree, not a newly invented school major. Application-level applicability may associate its subjects with multiple majors without cloning the educational nodes.

The source sometimes uses `فصل`, `بخش`, `درس`, `گفتار`, or `موضوع`, and sometimes uses an unlabeled branch. Preserve each displayed label. Assign a node type from its structural depth and context, not by translating or rewriting its label:

- an explicit `فصل` or `بخش` directly below a subject is normally a Chapter;
- an explicit `درس`, `گفتار`, or `موضوع` below a subject or chapter is normally a Topic;
- children below a topic are Concepts;
- deeper children are Sub-concepts;
- where the source omits an intermediate level, do not synthesize a placeholder node;
- where the parent or level is genuinely unclear, quarantine the row for review rather than guessing.

Cross-grade headings containing `موضوعی` are alternate source-defined organizations. They must remain distinct source trees until a future decision determines whether they are subjects, views, or taxonomies. They must not be merged automatically with grade-specific nodes merely because their labels are similar.

## Historical projection metadata: node semantics and ownership

| Level | Meaning | Owned by | Relationships | Change and version policy |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Curriculum root | The single entry point for one published curriculum release. The source does not provide an official display name for this root. | The curriculum release. | Owns field/major scopes. | Release metadata may change only by creating a new release. A published root is immutable. |
| Field / Major | A source-defined course scope: shared courses or one of the three named majors. | Curriculum root. | Owns grades; may have applicability relationships to shared subjects without changing ownership. | Rename, reorder, split, or merge only in a new version. Keep the old node available to historical versions. |
| Grade | One of `دهم`, `یازدهم`, or `دوازدهم` within a scope. | Field/major scope. | Owns the subjects listed in that scope and grade. | Versioned. A move between scopes is a relationship change in a new version, not an in-place historical edit. |
| Subject | A course title exactly as listed in the high-level matrix or an explicitly titled detailed/thematic tree. | Grade in the owning tree; unresolved cross-grade thematic trees remain quarantined pending classification. | Owns chapters or, when the source omits chapters, topics/concepts directly. | Versioned. A title correction does not change its stable Node ID; a change of educational identity creates a new node. |
| Chapter | A source grouping, commonly labeled `فصل` or `بخش`. | Subject. | Owns topics or concepts. | Versioned. It may be reordered or moved only in a new release. |
| Topic | A teachable subdivision, commonly labeled `درس`, `گفتار`, or `موضوع`. | Chapter, or Subject when no chapter exists in the source. | Owns concepts. | Versioned. Preserve source ordering and numbering text. |
| Concept | A directly assessable or teachable item explicitly present below a chapter/topic. | Topic, or Chapter/Subject when the source skips levels. | May own sub-concepts. May be referenced by learning records. | Versioned. Never reuse its ID for a different meaning. |
| Sub-concept | Any explicit source node below a concept; use recursively only as far as the source goes. | Its immediate parent concept or sub-concept. | Leaf or parent of another explicit sub-concept. | Versioned under the same rules as Concept. Do not create it merely to force a fixed depth. |

Ownership means one canonical parent edge per node per curriculum version. Reuse, prerequisite, similarity, applicability, and replacement are non-owning relationships and must not alter the source tree.

## Historical projection metadata: naming conventions

- `display_name` is the exact visible source text, including Persian/Arabic diacritics, digits, punctuation, stars (`⭐`), and English text. Do not silently correct spelling, spacing, or typography.
- Store text as Unicode and normalize to NFC for storage consistency. If search requires additional normalization, keep it in a separate derived search value; never overwrite `display_name`.
- Keep source numbering such as `فصل ۱`, `درس ۲`, and `Lesson 1` in the display name because it is part of the source label. Also store ordering separately when the data model is implemented.
- Node type names in architecture and interchange metadata are singular English values: `curriculum_root`, `field`, `grade`, `subject`, `chapter`, `topic`, `concept`, and `subconcept`. These are not user-facing translations.
- Names are not unique identifiers. The same title may occur in different grades, fields, or branches.
- Preserve source order with an explicit sibling position. Alphabetical sorting is not canonical order.
- Do not infer missing words, headings, parents, grade labels, or field labels from outside knowledge.

## Historical projection metadata: stable Node IDs

Each logical curriculum node receives one opaque, immutable Node ID on first accepted import. Recommended form: a lowercase type prefix plus UUIDv7, for example `sub_0195...` or `con_0195...`. Allowed prefixes are `cur`, `fld`, `grd`, `sub`, `chp`, `top`, `con`, and `sco` (sub-concept).

Rules:

- Generate the ID once; never derive it from a name, number, parent path, grade, or array position.
- A rename, reorder, or non-semantic move retains the Node ID but appears only in a new curriculum version.
- A split, merge, or change of educational identity creates new Node IDs and explicit lineage links.
- Node type is immutable. Reclassification creates a replacement node and lineage; it does not rewrite the old node.
- Every published reference resolves by the pair `(curriculum_version_id, node_id)`. The version selects the immutable snapshot; the Node ID identifies the logical node.
- A separate immutable revision/snapshot identifier may be used internally, but must not replace the public stable Node ID.
- Source locations and normalized paths are provenance and matching aids only; they are never IDs.

## Official high-level curriculum tree

```text
│   ├── حکمت متعالیه
│   ├── ملاصدرا
│   └── جریان‌های فلسفی متأخر
│

├── فلسفه معاصر اسلامی

└── جایگاه فلسفه در دوران معاصر

```

## Known source ambiguities and completeness limits

These issues require a future authorized curriculum decision before strict import:

1. The source explicitly says that some lower-priority lessons were not written out. Therefore the subject matrix is broader than the detailed trees; missing detail is not evidence that a subject or chapter does not exist.
2. The source provides no official name, academic year, ministry edition, publication date, edition number, or effective date for the curriculum root/version.
3. Detailed biology material begins immediately after the thematic English tree and contains three chapter-number sequences, but the source supplies no `زیست‌شناسی ۱`, `زیست‌شناسی ۲`, or `زیست‌شناسی ۳` headings at those boundaries. Parent assignment must be reviewed rather than inferred during strict import.
4. Several detailed areas omit intermediate levels or use different labels (`فصل`, `بخش`, `درس`, `گفتار`, `موضوع`, and unlabeled branches). Structural depth can be preserved, but exact semantic type mappings need approval where context is insufficient.
5. The cross-grade trees headed `کوریکولوم موضوعی عربی متوسطه دوم`, `کوریکولوم موضوعی فارسی متوسطه دوم`, `زبان انگلیسی کنکور (موضوعی)`, `ریاضی تجربی ـ کوریکولوم موضوعی`, and `علوم فنون موضوعی` do not state whether they are independent subjects, alternate views, or mappings to grade-specific nodes.
6. `مهارت‌ها و مباحث پیوندی ⭐` appears after the grade-specific chemistry trees without an explicit ownership declaration.
7. `شیمی دو رشته تجربی وریاضی مشترک است` is a prose relationship statement, not a clearly typed node, and needs a decision about shared ownership versus applicability.
8. The standalone headings `رشته ریاضی فیزیک` and `انسانی` are less formal than the high-level scope labels and need confirmed mapping to those scopes.
9. At least one Arabic paragraph combines two intended-looking tree entries in one paragraph (`عربی در ادبیات فارسی│   ├── فهم و ترجمه متن`). It is preserved verbatim and must not be split without authorization.
10. Stars (`⭐`) are present on selected labels, but their meaning is not defined. They remain part of display text until a future decision establishes separate metadata.
11. The source does not define prerequisites, learning objectives, hours, difficulty, assessment weights, aliases, equivalence mappings, or stable identifiers.

Until those decisions are made, the source transcript remains canonical and ambiguous rows remain quarantined; import tooling must not silently manufacture structure.
